        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Resident Discord Operator Tool Layer

## Overview
Add the missing resident Discord operator tools through `MegaplanResidentProfile`, preserving the current constrained model: allowlisted users can read/search, ordinary writes stay within existing editorial/store operations, and high-impact repo/cloud/reconciliation/export/archive actions require admin authorization plus exact confirmation where they can trigger broad side effects.

The brief’s hypothesis is mostly correct. The simplest path is not a new Discord persistence layer; it is a thin resident tool layer over the existing `Store` contract and existing cloud backend. The only foundational data gap is durable repo URL support: `Codebase` currently stores owner/name/default branch but not a clone URL, while cloud specs already support `repo.url`, `repo.branch`, and clone-if-missing in `megaplan/cloud/templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl`.

## Phase 1: Tool Surface And Security Foundation

### Step 1: Define resident input schemas and register tools (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`, `megaplan/resident/auth.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add focused `ToolInput` models for:
   - `search_messages`, `search_epics`, `search_plans`, `search_code_artifacts`
   - `list_codebases`, `list_repos`, `register_codebase`/`add_repo`
   - `read_plan_artifact`, `write_plan_artifact`
   - `reconcile_epic`, `reconcile_plan_storage`, `export_epic_bundle`, `archive_cloud_logs`
   - repo-aware `cloud_start_chain`/`cloud_bootstrap` glue where needed
2. Register them in `MegaplanResidentProfile._register_default_tools` alongside the existing resident tools.
3. Keep read/search registrations as `read` or `cloud_read`; register broad mutation/export/reconcile/archive/repo/cloud actions as `admin` or `cloud_start`.
4. Generalize confirmation only as much as needed: `ConfirmationManager.required_for` currently only covers `cloud_start`; add a small explicit high-impact action set or helper so repo mutation, export/archive, and reconciliation can opt into exact confirmation without weakening existing cloud behavior.

### Step 2: Add shared authorization/confirmation helpers (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Keep `_denied` as the single authorization gate.
2. Add a generic `_require_exact_confirmation(payload, action, tool_name, target_summary, request_id, phrase)` and have `_require_cloud_confirmation` delegate to it.
3. Ensure no handler creates store records, invokes cloud backend, writes artifacts, exports, or archives before authorization and confirmation pass.

## Phase 2: Store-Backed Read/Search Tools

### Step 3: Implement search and list handlers (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. `search_messages`: call `Store.search_messages(query=..., epic_id=..., limit=...)`.
2. `search_epics`: call `Store.search_epics(query=..., active_only=..., limit=...)`.
3. `search_plans`: use `Store.list_plans(...)` and apply simple in-memory matching over plan name, idea, state, and id; avoid adding a store method unless tests show this is too slow or inconsistent.
4. `search_code_artifacts`: use `Store.list_code_artifacts(...)` and apply simple matching over `file_path`, `content_summary`, `source`, and `content`.
5. `list_codebases` and `list_repos`: call `Store.list_codebases(...)`; `list_repos` can be an alias/shape focused on clone URL and branch.
6. Return `model_dump(mode="json")` data, limits capped by Pydantic fields, and no raw filesystem paths beyond existing store/cloud fields.

## Phase 3: Durable Repo/Codebase Records

### Step 4: Extend the Codebase schema minimally (`megaplan/schemas/arnold.py`, `megaplan/store/base.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `repo_url: str | None = None` to `Codebase`.
2. Add optional `repo_url` parameters to `Store.create_codebase` and `Store.upsert_codebase`.
3. Keep existing owner/name lowercase validation unchanged.
4. Validate clone URLs in the resident input layer rather than over-constraining the storage model; require a non-empty URL for resident `register_codebase`/`add_repo`.

### Step 5: Persist repo URL in FileStore, DBStore, MultiStore, and compatibility layers (`megaplan/store/file.py`, `megaplan/store/db.py`, `megaplan/store/multi.py`, `megaplan/store/compat.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Thread `repo_url` through FileStore create/upsert/update codebase paths.
2. Add `repo_url` to DB codebase column metadata, INSERT/UPSERT statements, conflict update fields, and row model construction.
3. Add the corresponding DB migration/schema update path used by this repository’s test setup.
4. Thread `repo_url` through MultiStore routing and compat JSON surfaces.
5. Update storage model tests and store contract tests to assert FileStore and DBStore preserve `repo_url`.

### Step 6: Implement repo registration/listing tools (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `register_codebase`/`add_repo`: admin + exact confirmation, then call `Store.upsert_codebase(..., repo_url=..., added_via="resident")` with deterministic idempotency.
2. `list_repos`: read-only, return only codebases with `repo_url` plus branch/scope/epic association.
3. Include enough response data for cloud startup to select `repo_url`, `default_branch`, and workspace.

## Phase 4: Plan Artifacts, Reconcile, Export, Archive

### Step 7: Add plan artifact tools (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. `read_plan_artifact`: read-only; load plan first, call `Store.read_plan_artifact`, and return UTF-8 text directly when decodable.
2. For binary artifacts, return base64 plus metadata instead of lossy text.
3. `write_plan_artifact`: admin + exact confirmation unless the project decides ordinary resident writes are acceptable; call `Store.write_plan_artifact` with `expected_revision` and deterministic idempotency.
4. Reuse existing plan artifact path safety from FileStore/DBStore instead of adding a parallel path validator.

### Step 8: Add reconciliation and export/archive handlers (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`, existing store/CLI modules)
**Scope:** Medium
1. `reconcile_epic`: wrap existing store/MultiStore reconciliation primitives if present; otherwise produce a read-only summary first and only perform writes behind admin + confirmation.
2. `reconcile_plan_storage`: use existing plan/artifact migration or reconciliation helpers already in `megaplan/store/multi.py` rather than duplicating copy logic.
3. `export_epic_bundle`: call the existing store export implementation if available (`megaplan/store/export.py` is the likely integration point); return artifact refs/paths and summary counts.
4. `archive_cloud_logs`: reuse `cloud_logs`/cloud run metadata and write logs as a plan or cloud-run artifact through Store-backed artifact APIs, with admin + exact confirmation.
5. Keep all these handlers free of arbitrary shell execution and unrestricted filesystem writes.

## Phase 5: Repo-Aware Cloud Start Glue

### Step 9: Connect registered repos to cloud bootstrap/start (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`, `megaplan/cloud/spec.py`, `megaplan/cloud/template.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add optional `codebase_id` or `repo` selector fields to cloud start/bootstrap inputs.
2. Resolve the selected `Codebase` before confirmation, and include `owner/name`, `repo_url`, and `default_branch` in the confirmation target summary.
3. After confirmation, pass repo URL/branch/workspace through the existing cloud spec/config path so `render_entrypoint` populates `REPO_URL` and `REPO_BRANCH`.
4. Reuse the existing entrypoint clone-if-missing block; only add resident-facing config glue if current cloud CLI/backend cannot accept repo overrides.
5. Do not add any Discord-facing shell or clone command tool.

## Phase 6: Tests And Validation

### Step 10: Add focused resident tests (`tests/test_resident_runtime_profile.py`, `tests/test_resident_cloud_tools.py`, new resident operator test file if useful)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Assert all new tool names are registered and shell/exec tools remain absent.
2. Assert allowlisted users can use read/search tools and blocked users cannot.
3. Assert non-admin users cannot register repos, write plan artifacts, reconcile, export, archive, or start cloud work.
4. Assert exact confirmation is required before side effects for repo registration, artifact write, reconciliation/export/archive, and cloud starts.
5. Assert search/list handlers return Store-backed data for messages, epics, plans, code artifacts, codebases, and repos.
6. Assert artifact read/write round-trips through FileStore and DBStore behavior where applicable.

### Step 11: Add storage/cloud tests (`tests/test_file_store.py`, `tests/test_db_store.py`, `tests/test_multi_store.py`, `tests/test_cloud_template.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Verify `Codebase.repo_url` persists in FileStore and DBStore and survives MultiStore routing/copy behavior.
2. Verify cloud template rendering still clones `REPO_URL`/`REPO_BRANCH` and existing cloud tests remain green.
3. Add a resident cloud test that selecting a registered repo passes repo URL/branch into the backend request without invoking side effects before confirmation.

## Execution Order
1. Start with schemas and tool registrations so tests can assert the public catalog early.
2. Add read/search handlers before write/admin handlers because they are cheap and clarify response shapes.
3. Land `Codebase.repo_url` storage changes before repo registration and cloud glue.
4. Add confirmation helper before implementing any new high-impact handler.
5. Implement artifact/reconcile/export/archive wrappers after the security helper is in place.
6. Wire repo-aware cloud starts last, reusing the existing cloud entrypoint behavior.

## Validation Order
1. Run targeted resident tests first: `pytest tests/test_resident_runtime_profile.py tests/test_resident_cloud_tools.py`.
2. Run targeted storage tests: `pytest tests/test_file_store.py tests/test_db_store.py tests/test_multi_store.py`.
3. Run targeted cloud template/spec tests: `pytest tests/test_cloud_template.py tests/test_cloud_spec.py`.
4. Run the broader suite or repository default test command after the focused checks pass.
5. Run lint/type checks if the repository has a standard command for them.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-029",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-v2"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-032",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-046",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-plan-to-extract-the-20260419-1637"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-050",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "vendor-hermes-agent-into-the-20260422-0112"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-064",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: batch execute: recurring debt: the revised step 11 still has an implementation-ordering problem for high-drift blocking in `handle_execute_one_batch`. in the real code, `blocking_reasons` is converted to `blocked` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:594-601`, and only later does the final-batch block build and write `execution.json` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:611-621`. the plan says to compute final-batch drift after that aggregate write but also append the high-drift reason before `blocked = bool(blocking_reasons)` is evaluated, which is impossible without explicitly moving/restructuring the existing `blocked` decision and the `state[\"current_state\"] = state_executed` assignment.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint1-step-receipts"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-067",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new judge auto-selection rule against the actual profile format. profiles use agent specs parsed by `parse_agent_spec()`, such as `claude`, `codex`, and `hermes:moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`; the plan's ordered auto candidates mix agent names (`claude`, `codex`) with a bare model name (`gpt-5`) and only says to scan execute/plan assignments for appearances. without a precise normalization rule, auto-selection can miss a compared profile using `hermes:openai/gpt-5` and can also hand `run_judge` a bare `claude` or `codex` token that is an agent label in this repo, not the same kind of model identifier as `gpt-5`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint2-bakeoff"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-073",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: stop-signal persistence is internally inconsistent. step 5 adds `stop_signal` to `execution_doc.json` task updates, but only adds `stance` to `finalize.json` tasks; step 11 then says `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)` reads latest `finalize.json` for `stance.stop_signal.requested`. in the current merge code, `_validate_and_merge_batch()` only copies fields listed in `merge_fields` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`), so a sibling `task_update.stop_signal` would not be present in `finalize.json` unless the plan explicitly adds it to the finalize schema and merge fields, or reads it from `execution.json`/`directors_notes.json` instead.",

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan adds _set_active_step and _clear_active_step helpers to handlers.py. _run_worker (handlers.py:135) is modified to accept a `resolved` parameter. but _run_worker is also called directly by handle_critique's sequential fallback at line 801 and 803. these calls currently don't pass `resolved`. the plan's step 2.6 for handle_critique says to set active_step after resolution at line 794 \u2014 but the _run_worker calls at 801/803 would re-resolve the agent internally (since `resolved` isn't passed). this means the agent is resolved twice on the critique sequential path. the double-resolution is wasteful but correct (same result). however, to match the plan's goal of 'resolve_agent_mode() is called once per handler invocation', the resolved tuple should be passed to these _run_worker calls too.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-058",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the location coverage is still not literally complete in the plan's validation layer because step 3.4 and step 9.1 only search for `from run_agent import` and `from hermes_state import` statements. that leaves bare `import run_agent` or `import hermes_state` outside `megaplan/agent/` unguarded even though the current repo already uses that form in availability helpers, so the 'all locations by construction' claim remains overstated.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "vendor-hermes-agent-into-the-20260422-0112"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-069",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked the added judge tests in step 13.6. they cover omitted, auto, and explicit judge branches, but the described auto test says profiles whose execute/plan map covers `claude` and `codex` should select `codex` or a third-tier model; per the ordered list and exclusion rule, `codex` is already used and should not be a valid auto-selected judge in that scenario.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint2-bakeoff"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-077",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: generated/runtime schema infrastructure is a required location. `ensure_runtime_layout()` writes every schema from `schemas` to `.megaplan/schemas` (`megaplan/_core/io.py:306-312`), and worker invocations read those disk schemas via `schemas_root()` (`megaplan/workers.py:1289-1290`, `1395-1396`; `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:281-283`). the plan updates `schemas` but does not include a runtime-schema assertion for `execution_doc.json`/creative mode or any new sidecar schema, so schema additions may not be tested through the same path agents actually consume.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-078",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan says to keep `--mode joke` working as an alias while new plans set `mode='creative'` and `form='joke'`, but it does not include a migration/helper contract for existing state files where `mode='joke'` and no `form` exists outside prompt dispatch. current callers read `state['config'].get('mode')` directly across execute, review, workers, and cli status; without a shared `is_creative_mode()`/`creative_form_id()` helper, it is easy for back-compat to work in prompts but fail in execution or validation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-086",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the workflow location coverage is still incomplete for creative-light. the plan updates `workflow_next()` behavior and tests only `workflow_next()` assertions, but the runnable command path also depends on `handle_finalize()`'s `require_state(..., {state_gated})` check in `megaplan/handlers/finalize.py:211-213`. the plan should either include that handler/state transition change or adjust the workflow design so `finalize` is only surfaced while the state is actually `state_gated`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i rechecked the caller paths after the revision. the raw-schema caller path through `validate_payload()` is now accounted for, but the runtime-schema caller path still is not: `strict_schema()` only reaches the on-disk runtime schemas through `ensure_runtime_layout()`, while current tests like `tests/test_schemas.py` and `tests/test_parallel_review.py` consume the already-written repo copy directly. the plan does not yet say how that caller path gets refreshed or asserted after the code change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]

    {
      "id": "DEBT-051",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the real callers for these paths are shell commands that expect actual files. reigh's readme uploads an idea file into `/workspace/megaplan-idea-2week.txt` before calling `megaplan init`, and `chain.sh` immediately checks `-f \"$spec\"` before running `megaplan chain --spec \"$spec\"`. the revised plan now constructs equivalent callers from `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec`, but still never adds the corresponding file-transfer or materialization step, so those callers would receive nonexistent arguments.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-065",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: batch execute callers still need a clearer identity contract. `handle_execute_one_batch()` can be invoked once per global batch in the same plan iteration, and the revised plan tells every invocation to use `phase=\"execute\"`, `iteration=state[\"iteration\"]`, and `attempt=1`. those actual caller arguments are not enough to distinguish batch receipts unless the plan changes the filename/attempt semantics or emits only one final aggregate receipt for `--batch` runs.",

      ]

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the planned judge resolver's inputs against `load_profiles()` and `parse_agent_spec()`. the caller will receive profile phase values as strings like `claude`, `codex`, or `hermes:glm-5.1`; to handle all actual values, the resolver should parse each spec into `(agent, model)` and compare canonical agent/model identities rather than doing raw string membership against `['claude', 'codex', 'gpt-5']`.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the mobile problem is broader than entrypage. `submissionscarouselpage` already has a pre-existing mobile gap because its score panel is desktop-only and its footer actions are also hidden on mobile (`src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347-354` plus `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`). the revised plan says entrypage should 'match the carouselpage pattern', which would spread that existing gap into the one page that currently has working mobile controls.",

      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: input provisioning is the broader unresolved concept here. the repo's current railway workflow does not just run commands; it also stages runtime artifacts into `/workspace` first, such as the uploaded idea text in readme line 103 and the expected `/workspace/chain.yaml` consumed by `chain.sh`. the revised plan fixes the runner dispatch itself, but it still treats `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec` as if those files will already exist without describing how cloud deploy makes that true.",

      "id": "DEBT-060",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: while the docstring on apply_profile_expansion correctly documents the recovery side, the root cause in megaplan/auto.py (_phase_command omitting --profile) has no comment explaining why the flag is intentionally dropped. a future maintainer might add --profile to the subprocess command without realizing it would make the state fallback redundant. however, adding --profile would be harmless because apply_profile_expansion would simply use args.profile directly, so no functional breakage would occur.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-062",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: execute receipt identity: the plan introduces a new collision for multi-batch `--batch` runs. it says execute emits one receipt per handler invocation and `attempt` stays 1, while step 11 emits non-final and final batch receipts with the same `phase=\"execute\"` and `iteration=state[\"iteration\"]`; the plan-dir filename remains `step_receipt_execute_v<iteration>.json`. running batch 1 and then batch 2 in the same iteration would overwrite the plan-dir receipt and create multiple jsonl rows with the same `(plan_id, phase, iteration, attempt)`, contradicting the brief's one-receipt-per-identity contract.",

      {
        "id": "DEBT-009",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-010",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
        ]

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Resident Discord Operator Tool Layer

## Overview
Add the missing resident Discord operator tools through `MegaplanResidentProfile`, preserving the current constrained model: allowlisted users can read/search, while broad mutation, export/archive, reconciliation, repo, and cloud-start operations require admin authorization and exact confirmation before side effects.

The critique flags do not indicate the plan targeted the wrong root cause. The core approach remains correct: resident tools should be thin wrappers over `Store`, existing export collection, and existing cloud backend surfaces. The needed revision is tighter execution design around security-sensitive boundaries: bounded export destinations, redacted code artifact search results, explicit high-impact confirmation action types, concrete registered-repo-to-`CloudSpec.repo` wiring, and a defined Store-backed target for archived cloud logs.

## Phase 1: Tool Surface And Security Foundation

### Step 1: Define resident input schemas and register tools (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`, `megaplan/resident/auth.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add focused `ToolInput` models for `search_messages`, `search_epics`, `search_plans`, `search_code_artifacts`, `list_codebases`, `list_repos`, `register_codebase`/`add_repo`, `read_plan_artifact`, `write_plan_artifact`, `reconcile_epic`, `reconcile_plan_storage`, `export_epic_bundle`, and `archive_cloud_logs`.
2. Add optional `codebase_id`, `repo_url`, `repo_branch`, and `repo_workspace` fields only to cloud start/bootstrap inputs that need registered repo selection.
3. Register all new tools in `MegaplanResidentProfile._register_default_tools`.
4. Keep read/search operations as `read` or `cloud_read`; classify repo mutation, artifact write, reconciliation, export, archive, and cloud starts as high-impact admin operations.
5. Assert no tool names expose shell, exec, remote shell, filesystem write, or arbitrary clone command capabilities.

### Step 2: Make high-impact confirmation explicit (`megaplan/resident/auth.py`, `megaplan/resident/config.py`, `megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `ActionKind` with explicit high-impact actions such as `repo_mutation`, `artifact_write`, `reconcile`, `export_bundle`, and `archive_logs`.
2. Update `ResidentAuthorizer.authorize_action` so `cloud_start`, `admin`, and the new high-impact actions require `admin_user_ids` membership after inbound allowlist checks.
3. Extend `ConfirmationManager.required_for` so `cloud_start` continues to use `require_cloud_start_confirmation`, and the new high-impact actions require exact confirmation by default through an explicit configured/default action set.
4. Add a generic `_require_exact_confirmation(payload, action, tool_name, target_summary, request_id, phrase)` helper and have `_require_cloud_confirmation` delegate to it.
5. Ensure handlers call authorization and confirmation before creating store records, invoking cloud backend, writing artifacts, exporting, archiving, or reconciling.

## Phase 2: Store-Backed Read/Search Tools

### Step 3: Implement message, epic, plan, and codebase read tools (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. `search_messages`: call `Store.search_messages(query=..., epic_id=..., limit=...)`.
2. `search_epics`: call `Store.search_epics(query=..., active_only=..., limit=...)`.
3. `search_plans`: use `Store.list_plans(...)` and apply bounded in-memory matching over plan id, name, idea, current state, and metadata summary fields.
4. `list_codebases`: call `Store.list_codebases(...)` and return durable codebase metadata.
5. `list_repos`: return only codebases with `repo_url`, shaped as repo URL, branch, workspace if configured, scope, owner/name, and associated epic.
6. Cap all result limits in Pydantic schemas and return compact response objects rather than unbounded model dumps.

### Step 4: Implement redacted code artifact search (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. `search_code_artifacts`: call `Store.list_code_artifacts(...)` and filter over `file_path`, `content_summary`, `source`, metadata, and bounded content snippets.
2. Never return full `CodeArtifact.content` from search results by default.
3. Return only `id`, `codebase_id`, `epic_id`, `kind`, `source`, `file_path`, `line_range`, `scope`, `content_summary`, bounded matched snippet, metadata keys or allowlisted metadata fields, timestamps, and expiry.
4. Add a separate future-facing read-by-id path only if needed; keep it out of this task unless tests require it, and require admin confirmation if full content would be exposed.

## Phase 3: Durable Repo/Codebase Records

### Step 5: Extend the Codebase schema minimally (`megaplan/schemas/arnold.py`, `megaplan/store/base.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `repo_url: str | None = None` to `Codebase`.
2. Add optional `repo_url` parameters to `Store.create_codebase` and `Store.upsert_codebase`.
3. Keep existing owner/name lowercase validation unchanged.
4. Validate resident-provided clone URLs in the resident input layer and require non-empty `repo_url` for `register_codebase`/`add_repo`.
5. Accept HTTPS and SSH Git URLs unless an existing cloud provider test proves one form is unsupported.

### Step 6: Persist repo URL in all store implementations (`megaplan/store/file.py`, `megaplan/store/db.py`, `megaplan/store/multi.py`, `megaplan/store/compat.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Thread `repo_url` through FileStore create/upsert/update codebase paths.
2. Add `repo_url` to DB codebase column metadata, schema/migration setup, INSERT/UPSERT statements, conflict update fields, and row model construction.
3. Thread `repo_url` through MultiStore routing, copy/migration helpers, and compatibility JSON surfaces.
4. Update storage model, FileStore, DBStore, MultiStore, and store contract tests to assert `repo_url` persists and survives routing/copy behavior.

### Step 7: Implement repo registration/listing tools (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `register_codebase`/`add_repo`: require `repo_mutation` admin authorization and exact confirmation, then call `Store.upsert_codebase(..., repo_url=..., added_via="resident")` with deterministic idempotency.
2. Include owner/name, repo URL, branch, scope, group, and associated epic in the confirmation target summary.
3. Return the persisted codebase after stripping no fields except any future credential-bearing metadata.
4. Keep `list_repos` read-only and bounded.

## Phase 4: Safe Artifacts, Export, Reconcile, Archive

### Step 8: Add plan artifact tools (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. `read_plan_artifact`: authorize as read, load the plan, call `Store.read_plan_artifact`, and return UTF-8 text only when decodable and under a bounded size limit.
2. For binary or oversized artifacts, return metadata plus base64 only when within a configured small response limit; otherwise return size/hash metadata and require a narrower follow-up.
3. `write_plan_artifact`: require `artifact_write` admin authorization and exact confirmation, then call `Store.write_plan_artifact` with `expected_revision` and deterministic idempotency.
4. Reuse existing Store/FileStore/DBStore artifact path safety and add tests proving unsafe artifact names remain rejected.

### Step 9: Implement constrained epic export (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`, `megaplan/store/export.py`, `megaplan/resident/config.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Use `collect_epic_export(store, epic_id, ...)` to collect export bytes without mutation.
2. Do not expose a caller-supplied output path in the resident tool schema.
3. Add a managed export root to resident config, defaulting to a fixed path such as `.megaplan/resident-exports`, and resolve every export under that root using a deterministic sanitized file name.
4. Call `write_epic_export_tar` only with that managed path after `export_bundle` admin authorization and exact confirmation.
5. Verify the resolved output path is inside the managed export root before writing.
6. Return export manifest summary, size, sha256, and managed relative path; never accept arbitrary absolute or `..` output paths from Discord.

### Step 10: Implement reconciliation wrappers (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`, `megaplan/store/multi.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. `reconcile_epic`: provide a read-only dry-run summary by default, using existing Store/MultiStore migration or reconciliation helpers where present.
2. Any write-applying reconciliation requires `reconcile` admin authorization and exact confirmation.
3. `reconcile_plan_storage`: reuse existing plan/artifact migration and copy verification helpers in `megaplan/store/multi.py`; do not duplicate plan artifact copy logic.
4. Return counts, copied ids, mismatches, warnings, and whether the operation was dry-run or applied.

### Step 11: Implement cloud log archiving with a defined Store target (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. `archive_cloud_logs` requires `archive_logs` admin authorization and exact confirmation.
2. Load the cloud run and determine the plan target as `payload.plan_id` or `cloud_run.plan_id`.
3. If no plan id is available, fail with `plan_id required for archive_cloud_logs`; do not invent a cloud-run artifact store in this task.
4. Invoke the existing constrained `cloud_logs` backend path with `no_follow=true` and persist captured stdout/details to `Store.write_plan_artifact(plan_id, "cloud-logs/<cloud_run_id>.json", ...)`.
5. Return the written `ArtifactRef`, cloud run id, plan id, and log byte/hash metadata.

## Phase 5: Concrete Repo-Aware Cloud Start Glue

### Step 12: Add cloud CLI repo override support (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py`, `megaplan/cloud/spec.py`, `megaplan/resident/cloud.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add shared cloud CLI options `--repo-url`, `--repo-branch`, and `--repo-workspace` to the cloud subcommands used by resident start/bootstrap where appropriate.
2. Update `_load_cloud_spec(root, args)` to load the existing cloud YAML and then return `replace(spec, repo=RepoSpec(url=override_url or spec.repo.url, branch=override_branch or spec.repo.branch, workspace=override_workspace or spec.repo.workspace))` when overrides are present.
3. Update resident cloud backend `_argv_for_request` to forward `repo_url`, `repo_branch`, and `repo_workspace` request arguments as those flags for `cloud_start_chain` and `cloud_bootstrap`.
4. Keep existing `cloud_yaml` behavior intact for callers that do not use registered repos.
5. Add tests proving the CLI override path changes the rendered `CloudSpec.repo` without modifying the source cloud YAML file.

### Step 13: Resolve registered codebases before cloud side effects (`megaplan/resident/profile.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a helper that resolves `codebase_id` or explicit repo selector fields to `Codebase.repo_url`, `default_branch`, and workspace.
2. Run this resolver before confirmation so the exact confirmation summary includes the actual repo URL, branch, and workspace that will be used.
3. After confirmation, pass `repo_url`, `repo_branch`, and `repo_workspace` into `_run_cloud_tool` arguments.
4. Confirm backend requests include repo override arguments before any cloud side effect is invoked.
5. Reuse the existing cloud entrypoint clone-if-missing behavior; do not add a Discord-facing clone/shell command.

## Phase 6: Tests And Validation

### Step 14: Add focused resident operator tests (`tests/test_resident_runtime_profile.py`, `tests/test_resident_cloud_tools.py`, new `tests/test_resident_operator_tools.py` if useful)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Assert all requested tools are registered and shell/exec tools remain absent.
2. Assert allowlisted users can use read/search tools and blocked users cannot.
3. Assert non-admin users cannot register repos, write artifacts, apply reconciliation, export, archive logs, or start cloud work.
4. Assert exact confirmation is required before side effects for repo registration, artifact write, reconciliation apply, export, archive logs, and cloud starts.
5. Assert code artifact search omits full `content` and returns only bounded snippets/summaries.
6. Assert export rejects arbitrary output paths by construction because no output path is accepted.
7. Assert `archive_cloud_logs` fails without a plan target and writes a plan artifact when a plan id is available.

### Step 15: Add storage and cloud tests (`tests/test_file_store.py`, `tests/test_db_store.py`, `tests/test_multi_store.py`, `tests/test_cloud_template.py`, `tests/test_cloud_chain_wrapper.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Verify `Codebase.repo_url` persists in FileStore and DBStore and survives MultiStore routing/copy behavior.
2. Verify cloud repo override flags produce `CloudSpec.repo.url`, branch, and workspace values passed through to rendered/deployed behavior.
3. Verify resident cloud start with a registered repo sends repo override arguments to the backend only after successful confirmation.
4. Verify existing cloud template clone-if-missing tests remain green.

## Execution Order
1. Land explicit high-impact action/confirmation handling first, before adding any side-effecting resident tools.
2. Add read/search tools next, including redacted code artifact response shaping.
3. Add `Codebase.repo_url` schema and store persistence before repo registration or cloud glue.
4. Implement repo registration/listing after persistence exists.
5. Implement safe artifact, export, reconciliation, and archive handlers with confirmation gates.
6. Add cloud CLI repo override support, then wire registered codebases into resident cloud start/bootstrap.
7. Finish with focused tests, then broader storage/cloud validation.

## Validation Order
1. Run resident authorization and profile tests first: `pytest tests/test_resident_runtime_profile.py tests/test_resident_cloud_tools.py tests/test_resident_operator_tools.py`.
2. Run storage persistence tests: `pytest tests/test_file_store.py tests/test_db_store.py tests/test_multi_store.py tests/test_store_contract_adapter.py`.
3. Run export/cloud targeted tests: `pytest tests/test_epic_cli.py tests/test_cloud_template.py tests/test_cloud_chain_wrapper.py tests/test_cloud_spec.py`.
4. Run the broader suite or repository default test command after focused checks pass.
5. Run lint/type checks if the repository has a standard command for them.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-029",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-v2"
      ]
    },
    {

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]

    {
      "id": "DEBT-046",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-plan-to-extract-the-20260419-1637"
      ]
    },

      "id": "DEBT-050",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "vendor-hermes-agent-into-the-20260422-0112"
      ]

    {
      "id": "DEBT-064",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: batch execute: recurring debt: the revised step 11 still has an implementation-ordering problem for high-drift blocking in `handle_execute_one_batch`. in the real code, `blocking_reasons` is converted to `blocked` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:594-601`, and only later does the final-batch block build and write `execution.json` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:611-621`. the plan says to compute final-batch drift after that aggregate write but also append the high-drift reason before `blocked = bool(blocking_reasons)` is evaluated, which is impossible without explicitly moving/restructuring the existing `blocked` decision and the `state[\"current_state\"] = state_executed` assignment.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      "id": "DEBT-067",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new judge auto-selection rule against the actual profile format. profiles use agent specs parsed by `parse_agent_spec()`, such as `claude`, `codex`, and `hermes:moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`; the plan's ordered auto candidates mix agent names (`claude`, `codex`) with a bare model name (`gpt-5`) and only says to scan execute/plan assignments for appearances. without a precise normalization rule, auto-selection can miss a compared profile using `hermes:openai/gpt-5` and can also hand `run_judge` a bare `claude` or `codex` token that is an agent label in this repo, not the same kind of model identifier as `gpt-5`.",

      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-072",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: stop-affordance: the brief's reason for the maker stop signal is to prevent additional passes from damaging the piece, but the plan says to halt further critique/revise cycles only when `stop_signal.requested=true` and the review verdict is approved. in the current review handler, an approved review already returns `state_done` with no next step in `_resolve_review_outcome()` (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:148-190`), so that condition is effectively a no-op and would not protect against a reviewer requesting rework despite the maker's stop defense.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-082",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: creative-light still does not satisfy the brief's intended single-pass path. the v3 plan proposes `workflow_next()` for creative-light at `state_planned` should return `finalize`, but `handle_finalize()` currently calls `require_state(state, \"finalize\", {state_gated})` in `megaplan/handlers/finalize.py:211-213`. since `workflow_next()` only reports a next step and does not mutate `current_state`, a plan left in `state_planned` would surface `finalize` and then fail the finalize handler's state check.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the prior missing locations in `megaplan/workers.py` and `tests/test_workers.py` are now covered, but one supporting-infrastructure path is still absent from the steps: the repo-root generated schema copy under `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and the tests that read it directly. without either explicitly regenerating that file or changing those tests to materialize schemas in a temp root, the plan can still leave the runtime-schema copy out of sync with the raw registry.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-035",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan adds _set_active_step and _clear_active_step helpers to handlers.py. _run_worker (handlers.py:135) is modified to accept a `resolved` parameter. but _run_worker is also called directly by handle_critique's sequential fallback at line 801 and 803. these calls currently don't pass `resolved`. the plan's step 2.6 for handle_critique says to set active_step after resolution at line 794 \u2014 but the _run_worker calls at 801/803 would re-resolve the agent internally (since `resolved` isn't passed). this means the agent is resolved twice on the critique sequential path. the double-resolution is wasteful but correct (same result). however, to match the plan's goal of 'resolve_agent_mode() is called once per handler invocation', the resolved tuple should be passed to these _run_worker calls too.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-megaplan-s-planning-20260409-2103"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-048",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: supporting infrastructure is still missing for mode inputs. step 3 ports `chain.yaml.example` into package templates, but step 5's `materialize_deploy_dir()` layout does not include it, step 7 deploy does not upload it or `auto.idea_file`, and the docs/validation steps do not mention any artifact-sync path before boot. that leaves the runner depending on files that the deployment plan never places on the container volume.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-058",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the location coverage is still not literally complete in the plan's validation layer because step 3.4 and step 9.1 only search for `from run_agent import` and `from hermes_state import` statements. that leaves bare `import run_agent` or `import hermes_state` outside `megaplan/agent/` unguarded even though the current repo already uses that form in availability helpers, so the 'all locations by construction' claim remains overstated.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "vendor-hermes-agent-into-the-20260422-0112"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-069",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked the added judge tests in step 13.6. they cover omitted, auto, and explicit judge branches, but the described auto test says profiles whose execute/plan map covers `claude` and `codex` should select `codex` or a third-tier model; per the ordered list and exclusion rule, `codex` is already used and should not be a valid auto-selected judge in that scenario.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint2-bakeoff"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-077",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: generated/runtime schema infrastructure is a required location. `ensure_runtime_layout()` writes every schema from `schemas` to `.megaplan/schemas` (`megaplan/_core/io.py:306-312`), and worker invocations read those disk schemas via `schemas_root()` (`megaplan/workers.py:1289-1290`, `1395-1396`; `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:281-283`). the plan updates `schemas` but does not include a runtime-schema assertion for `execution_doc.json`/creative mode or any new sidecar schema, so schema additions may not be tested through the same path agents actually consume.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-078",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan says to keep `--mode joke` working as an alias while new plans set `mode='creative'` and `form='joke'`, but it does not include a migration/helper contract for existing state files where `mode='joke'` and no `form` exists outside prompt dispatch. current callers read `state['config'].get('mode')` directly across execute, review, workers, and cli status; without a shared `is_creative_mode()`/`creative_form_id()` helper, it is easy for back-compat to work in prompts but fail in execution or validation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-086",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the workflow location coverage is still incomplete for creative-light. the plan updates `workflow_next()` behavior and tests only `workflow_next()` assertions, but the runnable command path also depends on `handle_finalize()`'s `require_state(..., {state_gated})` check in `megaplan/handlers/finalize.py:211-213`. the plan should either include that handler/state transition change or adjust the workflow design so `finalize` is only surfaced while the state is actually `state_gated`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "creative-work-mode"
      ]
    }

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i rechecked the caller paths after the revision. the raw-schema caller path through `validate_payload()` is now accounted for, but the runtime-schema caller path still is not: `strict_schema()` only reaches the on-disk runtime schemas through `ensure_runtime_layout()`, while current tests like `tests/test_schemas.py` and `tests/test_parallel_review.py` consume the already-written repo copy directly. the plan does not yet say how that caller path gets refreshed or asserted after the code change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-051",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the real callers for these paths are shell commands that expect actual files. reigh's readme uploads an idea file into `/workspace/megaplan-idea-2week.txt` before calling `megaplan init`, and `chain.sh` immediately checks `-f \"$spec\"` before running `megaplan chain --spec \"$spec\"`. the revised plan now constructs equivalent callers from `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec`, but still never adds the corresponding file-transfer or materialization step, so those callers would receive nonexistent arguments.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-065",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: batch execute callers still need a clearer identity contract. `handle_execute_one_batch()` can be invoked once per global batch in the same plan iteration, and the revised plan tells every invocation to use `phase=\"execute\"`, `iteration=state[\"iteration\"]`, and `attempt=1`. those actual caller arguments are not enough to distinguish batch receipts unless the plan changes the filename/attempt semantics or emits only one final aggregate receipt for `--batch` runs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint1-step-receipts"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-070",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the planned judge resolver's inputs against `load_profiles()` and `parse_agent_spec()`. the caller will receive profile phase values as strings like `claude`, `codex`, or `hermes:glm-5.1`; to handle all actual values, the resolver should parse each spec into `(agent, model)` and compare canonical agent/model identities rather than doing raw string membership against `['claude', 'codex', 'gpt-5']`.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the mobile problem is broader than entrypage. `submissionscarouselpage` already has a pre-existing mobile gap because its score panel is desktop-only and its footer actions are also hidden on mobile (`src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347-354` plus `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`). the revised plan says entrypage should 'match the carouselpage pattern', which would spread that existing gap into the one page that currently has working mobile controls.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-030",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the revised plan correctly narrows the schema-shape problem itself back to the six audited mismatches, but the generated runtime-schema consumer path is broader than the plan acknowledges: `tests/test_parallel_review.py` also reads the runtime `review.json` schema from `schemas_root(repo_root)`, so stale generated schema copies can affect more than the direct schema tests.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-049",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: input provisioning is the broader unresolved concept here. the repo's current railway workflow does not just run commands; it also stages runtime artifacts into `/workspace` first, such as the uploaded idea text in readme line 103 and the expected `/workspace/chain.yaml` consumed by `chain.sh`. the revised plan fixes the runner dispatch itself, but it still treats `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec` as if those files will already exist without describing how cloud deploy makes that true.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

    {
      "id": "DEBT-057",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the broader concept is now the validation surface rather than the runtime surface. hermes imports already appear in two non-execution helpers via bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`, so the plan's claim that the helper-plus-gate pattern is self-correcting across future codebase evolution is too broad unless the guard also accounts for bare imports, not just `from ... import ...` forms.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        "vendor-hermes-agent-into-the-20260422-0112"

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-060",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: while the docstring on apply_profile_expansion correctly documents the recovery side, the root cause in megaplan/auto.py (_phase_command omitting --profile) has no comment explaining why the flag is intentionally dropped. a future maintainer might add --profile to the subprocess command without realizing it would make the state fallback redundant. however, adding --profile would be harmless because apply_profile_expansion would simply use args.profile directly, so no functional breakage would occur.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-062",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: execute receipt identity: the plan introduces a new collision for multi-batch `--batch` runs. it says execute emits one receipt per handler invocation and `attempt` stays 1, while step 11 emits non-final and final batch receipts with the same `phase=\"execute\"` and `iteration=state[\"iteration\"]`; the plan-dir filename remains `step_receipt_execute_v<iteration>.json`. running batch 1 and then batch 2 in the same iteration would overwrite the plan-dir receipt and create multiple jsonl rows with the same `(plan_id, phase, iteration, attempt)`, contradicting the brief's one-receipt-per-identity contract.",

      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked the broader profile-selection concept against built-in profiles. `standard.toml` uses plain `claude`/`codex` agent specs, while `all-open.toml` uses `hermes:<provider/model>` specs; judge auto-selection needs to compare both agent labels and model ids if it is meant to avoid reusing a profile's executor model.",

      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: mode aliasing is broader than init and prompt builders: `rg` found mode-specific surfaces in cli status display (`megaplan/cli.py:304-307`), execute timeout auditing (`megaplan/execute/timeout.py:77-84`), review pre-check gating (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:283-286`), schema selection, execute merge, workers, and hermes. the plan's file list mentions some of these categories generically, but it does not call out that every prose-artifact check currently written as `doc|joke` needs a creative/form-aware helper.",

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```
